Text:"Does Scaling Toward AGI Depend on What System-1 Lets System-2 Execute? Qi Wang1, Yun Qu1, Lizhou Cai1, Heming Zou1, Haoang Chi1, Yiqin Lv1, Xiang Li1, Haiyan Feng1, Mengyu Ge2, Yize Geng2, Yuhang Jiang1, Yixiu Mao1, Xiangyang Ji1 1Department of Automation, Tsinghua University, Beijing, China 2Department of Computer Science, Tsinghua University, Beijing, China Progress toward artificial general intelligence (AGI) has largely relied on scaling resource-intensive executive, System-2, through larger models, more data and compute, longer reasoning, and more agents. We ask whether the next stage may come instead from co-designing it with System-1, lightweight, semantically aware predictor that evaluates candidate interactions before execution, so that System-2 acts only on those worth their cost. Our answer is qualified yes. We study conditional interaction, predictgateexecuteupdate loop: System-1 estimates each candidates utility, System-2 executes those admitted by an adaptive threshold, and the outcomes update System-1. The loop spans training, test-time reasoning, adaptive decision-making, and agentic tool use, with utility and cost defined by the application. Under ideal screening and equal execution costs, execution compute falls by factor approaching 1/𝜌𝑢, where 𝜌𝑢 is the density of utility-relevant interactions (cost-weighted when costs differ); prediction errors, exploration, and distribution shift reduce the saving. We bound selection regret by predictor error and the utility margin. These results motivate our central hypothesis: 𝜌𝑢 is measurable coordinate of scaling efficiency and makes informative compute better description of scaling than total compute. Date: October 27, 2026 Correspondence: Prof. Xiangyang Ji at xyji@tsinghua.edu.cn Project Website: https://cheemswang.github.io/blogs/sys1-sys2-codesign.html THU-IDM www.thuidm.com Recent advances toward artificial general intelligence (AGI) (Morris et al., 2024) have largely focused on scaling model capacity"

Text:"Wizard: Manipulate Any World in Real Time via Agentic Control Yuzhe Wu1,2, Zijun Lin1,3, Yeying Jin1,2, 1 Tencent 2 NUS 3 NTU Recent advances in video world models have enabled the generation of increasingly realistic and interactive virtual environments, opening new possibilities for player-customizable gameplay. Agentic frameworks further advance this direction by decoupling explicit game logic and controllable world representations from visual generation. However, existing approaches support only simple game settings and predefined motion patterns, generalize poorly to unseen environments and characters, and offer limited real-time interactivity. In this paper, we introduce Wizard, an agentic game world model that enables real-time, precise control over character motions while generalizing zero-shot across novel scenes, characters, and actions. To achieve this, we construct diverse dataset spanning 750 scenes and 178 characters, incorporating skeleton and geometry information as structured representations. We leverage these representations as intermediate proxies to condition video generation model, together with an adaptive masking mechanism that improves alignment between the conditioning signals and generated video rollouts. Furthermore, we introduce an agentic framework that explicitly maintains and updates game states, allowing players to freely configure their virtual worlds and exercise fine-grained control over character motions while preserving visual quality and gameplay consistency. By combining agentic control with structured proxy-based generation, our framework unlocks new possibilities for player-driven game creation with real-time, generalizable, and mechanically consistent world simulation. We present Apriel Voice 27B Preview, an open speech-language model that unifies these abilities into shared reasoning backbone while retaining text and image input modalities. Built on the post-trained Gemma 4 26B-A4B checkpoint, Apriel Voice processes speech directly and generates both responses and tool calls without requiring separate dialogue model. two-stage training procedure adapts the model to speech. Stage 1 establishes the speech interface. Stage 2 jointly adapts the projector and backbone using supervised fine-tuning, text replay, and source-dependent cross-entropy and forward KullbackLeibler divergence supervision. Automatically constructed speech-conditioned dialogues provide conversational training data. Local teacher serving, overlapping teacher and student computation, and fused full-vocabulary losses enable effective online distillation. In thinking mode, Apriel Voice scores 98.90 on Big Bench Audio, 81.13 on Speech-BFCL, and 65.51% task success on τ -Voice, with mean latency of 1.91 seconds. English EVA accuracy and experience pass@1 reach 51.27% and 74.13%. We serve Apriel Voice 27B Preview through custom platform to enable interactive voice interactions. Text:"2026-11-02 REA: Routed Embodied Agent Runtime for Asynchronous Brain-Cerebellum Control Zhengfeiyang Zhang2,4 Haoyuan Chen2,5 Pengfei Zhou1,2 Yixing Ma2,3 Zhenglin Wan1 Hexin Wang2 Wangbo Zhao6 Yang You1 1National University of Singapore, HPC-AI Lab 2Inf Rec, Cardinal AI Lab 3University of California, Berkeley 4The University of Tokyo 5Shenzhen Institutes of Advanced Technology, Chinese Academy of Sciences 6Hong Kong University of Science and Technology # wanghexin@infrec.tech; # wangbo.zhao96@gmail.com; # yangyou@nus.edu.sg Corresponding Author Project page: https://mikeeeeeeeeeeeeee.github.io/REA/ Abstract Foundation models are becoming remarkably reliable at high-level reasoning, yet they remain too slow and costly to serve as fine-grained controllers. Conversely, fast policies like VLAs and whole-body controllers offer more efficient reflexes, but struggle to generalize. While pairing deliberative brain (foundation models) with reactive cerebellum (fast policies) seems natural for embodied systems, two obstacles remain: methods are built and scored on incompatible stacks, and their asynchronous coordination is difficult. For example, an agent that calls the VLA in the agentic loop can stall the fast execution, while VLA that calls the agent on errors tends to re-trigger fallbacks repeatedly or completely miss silent failures. To address this, we propose REA (Routed Embodied Agent), unified runtime where deliberative System 2, an embodied router System 1 and reactive System 0 policies cooperate under single golden rule: no running policy waits on the brain. Instead of querying the brain at each failure, REA employs route memory that stores each policys measured success ahead of time, boundary-triggered judge gate that consults the brain on policy execution, and shared-budget escalation ladder for bounded recovery. Evaluated on 3,000 test episodes across 60 LIBERO-Pro tasks, REA reaches 95.9% success and beats the best single-agent baseline in its own policy pool "

Existing LLM-based methods have improved rewriting by using synthetic reasoning and subsequent consumptionbehavioral feedback. However, overemphasizing subsequent consumption can favor engaging content over the users immediate need, causing intent drift even when rewritten queries are grounded in behavioral history. We propose WeFair, Fidelity-Aware Alignment from Human Feedback for Personalized Query Rewriting in WeChat short-video search. WeFair uses human feedback according to how directly it reflects personalized intent, assigning feedback fidelity to progression from demonstration to preference to verification. We first mine actual queries and user reformulations from search sessions, and then screen them for contextual support. In the demonstration stage, supervised fine-tuning curriculum progresses from large-scale context-to-query learning to reasoning and direct prediction on human reformulation and preservation. In the preference stage, direct preference optimization learns relative preferences from subsequent consumption feedback. In the verification stage, group relative policy optimization rewards exact agreement with reformulation or preservation targets to constrain intent drift. Despite substantial progress, unified anti-forgery foundation model covering such broad threat spectrum remains absent. In this paper, we present Venus-DefakerOne2, which substantially extends the forensic capability boundary along both the forgery-form and media-type dimensions. Trained on tens of millions of open-source and in-house samples, it incorporates two key designs. Tide-Style-Tuning alternates LoRA and full-parameter fine-tuning to balance computational efficiency, generalization, and hard-sample fitting while mitigating catastrophic forgetting. Entropy-based Test Adaptation determines the optimal prediction modality by analyzing prediction entropy. Venus-DefakerOne2 achieves new state-of-theart performance across comprehensive suite of benchmarks, including DeepFake faces (96.7%), AI-generated images (93.3%), tampered documents (87.6%), manipulated natural images (89.6%), physical spoofs (93.1%), and AI-generated videos (96.0%). Split-channel large-kernel blocks preserve spatial context with limited computation, while branch fusion removes redundant inference paths. Ternary Progressive Quantization-aware Training (TPQ) then adapts the fused network from deep to shallow layers, through the decoder, and finally to the input and prediction layers. All 25 convolutions use ternary weights and 8-bit activations, enabling packed storage and multiplier-free computation. Abstract Co-designing a resource-heavy reasoning system with a lightweight predictive evaluator can reduce execution costs by filtering interactions, with efficiency governed by the density of useful interactions.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Progress toward artificial general intelligence (AGI) has largely relied on scaling a resource-intensive executive, System-2, through larger models, more data and compute, longer reasoning, and more agents. We ask whether the next stage may come instead from co-designing it with System-1, a lightweight, semantically aware predictor that evaluates candidate interactions before execution, so that System-2 acts only on those worth their cost. Our answer is a qualified yes. We study conditional interaction, a predict--gate--execute--update loop: System-1 estimates each candidate's utility, System-2 executes those admitted by an adaptive threshold, and the outcomes update System-1. The loop spans training, test-time reasoning, adaptive decision-making, and agentic tool use, with utility and cost defined by the application. Under ideal screening and equal execution costs, execution compute falls by a factor approaching 1/ρ_u, where ρ_u is the density of utility-relevant interactions (cost-weighted when costs differ); prediction errors, exploration, and distribution shift reduce the saving. We bound selection regret by predictor error and the utility margin. These results motivate our central hypothesis: ρ_u is a measurable coordinate of scaling efficiency and makes informative compute a better description of scaling than total compute.
[02.11.2026 09:35] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает совместно проектировать тяжёлую систему рассуждений System-2 и лёгкий предсказатель System-1, который оценивает кандидатов на взаимодействие до их выполнения. Петля predict–gate–execute–update пропускает к System-2 только те взаимодействия, полезность которых превышает адаптивный порог, а результаты обновляют предсказатель. Теоретически показано, что вычислительные затраты падают примерно в 1/ρ_u раз, где ρ_u — плотность полезных взаимодействий, а ошибки предсказания и исследование снижают эффект, при этом регрет ограничен ошибкой предсказателя и полезным запасом. Авторы утверждают, что ρ_u можно измерять и использовать как показатель эффективности масштабирования, полезного для AGI, вместо простого роста общего объёма вычислений.", "emoji": "🧠", "title": "Считать не всё подряд: умный отбор вместо грубой мощности"}

Abstract R²Orch uses recursive self-play with learnable task selection and skill withdrawal to train orchestrators without hand-designed curricula, improving performance across reasoning and interactive tasks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small In recent years, agentic orchestration has been widely applied to solve complex human tasks. However, existing orchestration learning still faces key challenges, including hand-designed curricula, advantage collapse when a task's rollouts all reach the same outcome, and unreliable evaluation signals that judge each skill by one noisy score. To address these challenges, we propose R^2Orch, a new paradigm of Recursive Self-Play Orchestration: an inner loop of self-play that improves the orchestrator on self-chosen tasks, and an outer loop that improves how the inner loop improves. To support this paradigm, we introduce Recursive Learnability, a training objective that rewards a Proposer for choosing tasks whose rollouts keep reaching different outcomes as skills are withdrawn. Built on this objective, we further propose Skill Withdrawal, in which the outer loop withdraws each skill once a controlled evaluation within self-play shows that the orchestrator performs as well without it. Moreover, the two loops form one cycle: failures are distilled into skills, skills into parameters, and withdrawals lead the Proposer to new failures, so the orchestrator moves toward zero-skill deployment. Experimental results on twelve datasets show that R^2Orch consistently outperforms baselines across question answering, math, code, and interactive tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/beita6969/R2Orch.
[02.11.2026 09:35] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает R²Orch — парадигму рекурсивного самообучения (Recursive Self-Play) для обучения оркестратора агентных LLM без вручную заданных учебных программ. Внутренний цикл self-play улучшает оркестратор на задачах, которые сам Proposer выбирает, а внешний цикл улучшает сам процесс обучения. Для этого вводится цель Recursive Learnability, поощряющая выбор задач, на которых траектории (rollouts) дают разные исходы даже после удаления навыков, а также механизм Skill Withdrawal, убирающий навыки, которые больше не нужны оркестратору. Эксперименты на двенадцати датасетах показывают, что метод стабильно превосходит базовые подходы в вопросах-ответах, математике, коде и интерактивных задачах.", "emoji": "🧠", "title": "Оркестратор учится сам и постепенно отказывается от подсказок"}

Abstract FrameForge enables adaptive conditional image generation by modeling variable condition-target relationships through dynamic temporal experts and intermediate reasoning pathways in pretrained video models.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Unified conditional image generation requires a single model to preserve different aspects of a visual input while producing a target image that follows textual guidance. Pretrained video models provide a promising foundation by encoding visual transformations across time, yet existing approaches typically assign the condition and target to fixed temporal positions. We observe that condition-target relationships vary substantially across generation tasks, while video models naturally encode visual transformations at different temporal scales. This motivates us to model conditional generation through input-adaptive virtual temporal relations. We propose FrameForge, which captures two complementary aspects of such relations. Mixture of Temporal Experts (MoTE) models how far the target lies from the condition by dynamically selecting temporal RoPE experts with different virtual intervals. Chain of Temporal Reasoning (CoTR) models how the condition is transformed into the target through a latent intermediate state, with a reasoning-bottleneck attention mask encouraging information propagation through this pathway. Experiments on DreamBench, COCO2017, and GEdit-Bench show that FrameForge achieves superior performance in subject-driven generation, canny-to-image generation, and image editing among the compared methods.

Mixture of Temporal Experts (MoTE) динамически выбирает RoPE-экспертов с разными виртуальными интервалами, а Chain of Temporal Reasoning (CoTR) моделирует преобразование условия в цель через промежуточное латентное состояние с attention-маской типа reasoning-bottleneck. Abstract Wizard is an agentic game world model that uses structured motion proxies and adaptive masking to enable real-time, generalizable video generation with precise character control across diverse scenes.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Recent advances in video world models have enabled the generation of increasingly realistic and interactive virtual environments, opening new possibilities for player-customizable gameplay. Agentic frameworks further advance this direction by decoupling explicit game logic and controllable world representations from visual generation. However, existing approaches support only simple game settings and predefined motion patterns, generalize poorly to unseen environments and characters, and offer limited real-time interactivity. In this paper, we introduce Wizard, an agentic game world model that enables real-time, precise control over character motions while generalizing zero-shot across novel scenes, characters, and actions. To achieve this, we construct a diverse dataset spanning 750 scenes and 178 characters, incorporating skeleton and geometry information as structured representations. We leverage these representations as intermediate proxies to condition a video generation model, together with an adaptive masking mechanism that improves alignment between the conditioning signals and generated video rollouts. Furthermore, we introduce an agentic framework that explicitly maintains and updates game states, allowing players to freely configure their virtual worlds and exercise fine-grained control over character motions while preserving visual quality and gameplay consistency. By combining agentic control with structured proxy-based generation, our framework unlocks new possibilities for player-driven game creation with real-time, generalizable, and mechanically consistent world simulation.

Abstract HiFOR improves robotic manipulation by hierarchically predicting future states and retrieving relevant future information to guide action generation.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have demonstrated promising capabilities in robotic manipulation by integrating visual perception, language understanding, and action generation. Recent advances have further incorporated future prediction to enhance the understanding of environmental dynamics and task progression. However, existing methods often model short-term dynamics and long-term task objectives separately, while directly incorporating predicted futures into action policies without sufficiently considering their relevance to current action requirements. To address these limitations, we propose HiFOR, a hierarchical foresight framework with action-guided world retrieval. Specifically, Hierarchical Foresight Prediction jointly predicts short-term state evolution, semantic milestones, and final goal states through a Hierarchical Dependency Mask (HDM), establishing dependencies across temporal horizons. Building upon these predictions, Structure-Aware Latent World Modeling organizes future latent representations along the horizon, view, and spatial dimensions to facilitate fine-grained retrieval. Furthermore, Action-Guided Factorized World Retrieval adaptively retrieves relevant future information from the structured latent world based on evolving action representations during denoising, providing targeted guidance for action generation. Extensive experiments on two simulation benchmarks and six real-world robotic manipulation tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach.

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract HiFOR improves robotic manipulation by hierarchically predicting future states and retrieving relevant future information to guide action generation.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models have demonstrated promising capabilities in robotic manipulation by integrating visual perception, language understanding, and action generation. Recent advances have further incorporated future prediction to enhance the understanding of environmental dynamics and task progression. However, existing methods often model short-term dynamics and long-term task objectives separately, while directly incorporating predicted futures into action policies without sufficiently considering their relevance to current action requirements. To address these limitations, we propose HiFOR, a hierarchical foresight framework with action-guided world retrieval. Specifically, Hierarchical Foresight Prediction jointly predicts short-term state evolution, semantic milestones, and final goal states through a Hierarchical Dependency Mask (HDM), establishing dependencies across temporal horizons. Building upon these predictions, Structure-Aware Latent World Modeling organizes future latent representations along the horizon, view, and spatial dimensions to facilitate fine-grained retrieval. Furthermore, Action-Guided Factorized World Retrieval adaptively retrieves relevant future information from the structured latent world based on evolving action representations during denoising, providing targeted guidance for action generation. Extensive experiments on two simulation benchmarks and six real-world robotic manipulation tasks demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach."

Abstract CEPO improves multi-agent prompt optimization by separating shared coordination from agent-specific execution, boosting performance across benchmarks and scaling efficiently with more agents.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Multi-agent systems (MAS) require both effective coordination and specialized task execution. Existing prompt optimization approaches typically couple these functions within agent-specific prompts. We introduce Coordinator-Executor Prompt Optimization (CEPO), a general framework for improving prompt optimization in MAS. CEPO separates system-level coordination from agent-level execution through a shared coordinator prompt and agent-specific executor prompts, and guides their updates according to their respective roles. This structure allows shared coordination rules to be refined consistently across agents while keeping execution instructions separately adjustable. CEPO provides the prompt structure and optimization guidance, while existing prompt optimizers perform the underlying search. Extensive experiments spanning 11 benchmarks across four domains demonstrate that applying CEPO to four existing prompt optimizers consistently improves their performance. Ablations and qualitative analyses support the proposed design, while controlled studies on a document-understanding MAS further demonstrate improved optimization budget efficiency and performance scaling with the number of agents.

Abstract LARC enables continuous latent reasoning for unified multimodal models, improving image generation quality and enabling test-time scaling without discrete token bottlenecks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Unified Multimodal Models (UMMs) achieve remarkable success on diverse multimodal understanding and generation tasks, yet they still struggle to effectively leverage language-based chain-of-thought reasoning for image generation. As a result, existing reasoning-augmented UMMs often fail to inherit the test-time scaling and fine-grained controllability observed in large language models. We attribute this failure to a fundamental modality mismatch: reasoning is performed in a discrete token space, whereas image generation operates in a continuous space. To bridge this gap, we introduce a Latent Reasoning framework in Continuous space (LARC), which alleviates the token-level bottleneck by reasoning over continuous hidden states. Specifically, the training framework consists of two stages: (i) curriculum supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and (ii) information-gain reinforcement learning (RL). First, the curriculum SFT stage gradually converts token-level reasoning into latent reasoning. Subsequently, the information-gain RL stage self-evolves latent reasoning without ground truth supervision. Experiments across diverse text-to-image generation benchmarks show that LARC consistently improves generation quality, prompt following, and compositional fidelity. Notably, LARC achieves state-of-the-art performance on GenEval with a 7%p gain over BAGEL and demonstrates clear test-time scaling behavior, outperforming token-based generation under both parallel and sequential test-time scaling strategies.
[02.11.2026 09:36] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет LARC — фреймворк латентного рассуждения в непрерывном пространстве для унифицированных мультимодальных моделей (UMM), который устраняет разрыв между дискретным текстовым рассуждением и непрерывной генерацией изображений. Обучение состоит из двух этапов: куррикулум-SFT, постепенно переводящий токенное рассуждение в латентное, и обучение с подкреплением на основе information-gain (RL), которое развивает латентное рассуждение без ground truth разметки. Эксперименты на бенчмарках text-to-image показывают улучшение качества генерации, соблюдения промпта и композиционной точности, а также результат SOTA на GenEval (+7 п.п. над BAGEL). Paper text to classify:

"Abstract LARC enables continuous latent reasoning for unified multimodal models, improving image generation quality and enabling test-time scaling without discrete token bottlenecks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Unified Multimodal Models (UMMs) achieve remarkable success on diverse multimodal understanding and generation tasks, yet they still struggle to effectively leverage language-based chain-of-thought reasoning for image generation. As a result, existing reasoning-augmented UMMs often fail to inherit the test-time scaling and fine-grained controllability observed in large language models. We attribute this failure to a fundamental modality mismatch: reasoning is performed in a discrete token space, whereas image generation operates in a continuous space. To bridge this gap, we introduce a Latent Reasoning framework in Continuous space (LARC), which alleviates the token-level bottleneck by reasoning over continuous hidden states. Specifically, the training framework consists of two stages: (i) curriculum supervised fine-tuning (SFT), and (ii) information-gain reinforcement learning (RL). First, the curriculum SFT stage gradually converts token-level reasoning into latent reasoning. Subsequently, the information-gain RL stage self-evolves latent reasoning without ground truth supervision. Experiments across diverse text-to-image generation benchmarks show that LARC consistently improves generation quality, prompt following, and compositional fidelity. Notably, LARC achieves state-of-the-art performance on GenEval with a 7%p gain over BAGEL and demonstrates clear test-time scaling behavior, outperforming token-based generation under both parallel and sequential test-time scaling strategies."

Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. Abstract CaPtain trains small open vision-language models via supervised fine-tuning and multi-turn reinforcement learning with a physical critic to perform robot manipulation through code, achieving strong results without frontier models or vision-language-action backbones.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Across coding, web, and tool-use tasks, language-model agents are now routinely trained with the same recipe: supervised fine-tuning (SFT) to learn the interface, then multi-turn reinforcement learning (RL) to master it. Code-as-policy (CaP) casts robot manipulation in the same form, as the policy writes code against robot APIs and reads back the results, so the same recipe should apply. Yet existing CaP agents sidestep training: they either prompt closed frontier models or delegate manipulation to pre-trained vision-language-action models (VLAs) and high-level skills, while small open vision-language models (VLMs) fail outright. We present CaPtain, an open post-training pipeline that turns a small open VLM into a capable CaP agent, with no VLA, frontier model, or privileged state at test time. CaPtain trains and deploys the policy inside CaPtain-Harness, which exposes low-level perception, geometry, and guarded-motion primitives across simulation and real hardware. We first align the base model via SFT on synthetic trajectories generated with GPT-6 Astra, then apply multi-turn RL. Multi-turn RL must credit tens of agent requests, whose physical effects often surface turns later, from a single end-of-episode outcome. We therefore introduce a physical critic that reads privileged simulator state, never seen by the policy, and credits each request by how much it changes the predicted chance of success. Starting from Qwen3.5-4B (0% zero-shot), CaPtain-4B reaches 55.4% held-out LIBERO success after SFT and 73.8% after RL. On LIBERO-PRO, it reaches 78.5%, surpassing several frontier-model agents, and its RL gains persist across three perturbation suites (+17.6 to +21.2 points). Without target-domain training, it attains 83.3% on SimplerEnv WidowX, exceeds frontier models on CaP-Bench, and runs on a real robot after simulation-only adaptation. We release all code, data, and model weights.

Модель сначала обучается через supervised fine-tuning на синтетических траекториях, а затем проходит multi-turn reinforcement learning. Ключевая новинка — physical critic, который использует приватное состояние симулятора, недоступное политике, и оценивает вклад каждого запроса в изменение предсказанной вероятности успеха. Итог: модель Qwen3.5-4B после обучения достигает 73.8% успеха на LIBERO и 83.3% на SimplerEnv без обучения на целевом домене, превосходя ряд агентов на frontier-моделях, и работает на реальном роботе.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Маленькая открытая VLM учится управлять роботом через код и сама превосходит гигантов"}

Abstract Apriel Voice 27B Preview is an open speech-language model that integrates speech understanding, reasoning, and tool use within a shared backbone, achieving strong results on audio benchmarks through a two-stage training procedure with online distillation.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small A capable voice assistant must do more than recognize words; it must understand requests, reason over context, sustain conversations, and act through tools. We present Apriel Voice 27B Preview, an open speech-language model that unifies these capabilities within a shared reasoning backbone while retaining text and image input modalities. Built on the post-trained Gemma 4 26B-A4B checkpoint, Apriel Voice directly processes speech and generates both responses and tool calls without requiring a separate dialogue model. We introduce a two-stage training procedure to adapt the model to speech. The first stage establishes the speech interface, while the second jointly adapts the projector and backbone through supervised fine-tuning, text replay, and source-dependent cross-entropy and forward Kullback--Leibler divergence supervision. Automatically constructed speech-conditioned dialogues provide conversational training data, while local teacher serving, overlapping teacher and student computation, and fused full-vocabulary losses enable efficient online distillation. In thinking mode, Apriel Voice achieves a score of 98.90 on Big Bench Audio, 81.13 on Speech-BFCL, and a task success rate of 65.51% on τ-Voice, with a mean latency of 1.91 seconds. It also achieves 51.27% English EVA accuracy and 74.13% experience pass@1. Finally, we deploy Apriel Voice 27B Preview on a custom serving platform to support interactive voice interactions.

Модель построена на основе Gemma 4 26B-A4B и напрямую обрабатывает речевой вход, генерируя ответы и вызовы инструментов без отдельной диалоговой LLM. "Abstract Apriel Voice 27B Preview is an open speech-language model that integrates speech understanding, reasoning, and tool use within a shared backbone, achieving strong results on audio benchmarks through a two-stage training procedure with online distillation.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small A capable voice assistant must do more than recognize words; it must understand requests, reason over context, sustain conversations, and act through tools. We present Apriel Voice 27B Preview, an open speech-language model that unifies these capabilities within a shared reasoning backbone while retaining text and image input modalities. Built on the post-trained Gemma 4 26B-A4B checkpoint, Apriel Voice directly processes speech and generates both responses and tool calls without requiring a separate dialogue model. We introduce a two-stage training procedure to adapt the model to speech. The first stage establishes the speech interface, while the second jointly adapts the projector and backbone through supervised fine-tuning, text replay, and source-dependent cross-entropy and forward Kullback--Leibler divergence supervision. Automatically constructed speech-conditioned dialogues provide conversational training data, while local teacher serving, overlapping teacher and student computation, and fused full-vocabulary losses enable efficient online distillation. In thinking mode, Apriel Voice achieves a score of 98.90 on Big Bench Audio, 81.13 on Speech-BFCL, and a task success rate of 65.51% on τ-Voice, with a mean latency of 1.91 seconds. It also achieves 51.27% English EVA accuracy and 74.13% experience pass@1. Finally, we deploy Apriel Voice 27B Preview on a custom serving platform to support interactive voice interactions."

Abstract WorldBridge improves robot task generalization by combining video prediction with sparse action supervision via separate video and action experts, achieving state-of-the-art results across manipulation benchmarks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Achieving the broad task generalization of embodied models in physical environments remains a central challenge in robot learning. We study this challenge in World Action Models (WAMs) through two complementary capabilities: video generalization, which predicts the visual evolution of new tasks, and action generalization, which translates these predictions into executable behavior through a video-to-action bridge. By varying video and action supervision for tasks outside an initial training set, we examine their respective contributions to task transfer. On RoboTwin 2.0, using only in-distribution video-action supervision yields a 25.07% success rate on out-of-distribution tasks. Introducing videos of these tasks without their action labels raises success to 55.90%, while adding just one action-labeled demonstration per task further improves it to 70.70%. These findings suggest that broader video coverage enables task transfer by leveraging existing inverse-dynamics capabilities, while even sparse action supervision further strengthens those capabilities. Guided by this perspective, we introduce WorldBridge, a WAM with separate video and action experts connected through structured attention and trained with joint flow matching. WorldBridge incorporates visual memory at two temporal scales and action forcing, alongside a unified data processing pipeline that standardizes and filters heterogeneous robot trajectories and egocentric hand-derived supervision. Across multiple manipulation benchmarks, WorldBridge achieves state-of-the-art performance among the compared methods, including 96.23% on RoboTwin 2.0 full data setting and 80.9% on clean to random setting, 82.8% on RoboCasa GR1 TableTop, and 57.6% on DOMINO Level-1. Our study highlights the complementary roles of video prediction and action grounding in developing robots that generalize across tasks.

Авторы показывают, что видео без разметки действий и даже одна размеченная демонстрация на задачу заметно повышают успешность переноса на out-of-distribution задачи. На основе этого предложен WorldBridge: WAM с отдельными видео- и action-экспертами, связанными structured attention и обученными совместным flow matching, с визуальной памятью на двух масштабах времени и унифицированным пайплайном обработки данных. Abstract REA unifies deliberative and reactive policies in a single runtime with asynchronous coordination, achieving high success rates with reduced control overhead across simulated and real robot tasks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Foundation models are becoming remarkably reliable at high-level reasoning, yet they remain too slow and costly to serve as fine-grained controllers. Conversely, fast policies like VLAs and whole-body controllers offer more efficient reflexes, but struggle to generalize. While pairing a deliberative brain (foundation models) with a reactive cerebellum (fast policies) seems natural for embodied systems, two obstacles remain: methods are built and scored on incompatible stacks, and their asynchronous coordination is difficult. For example, an agent that calls the VLA in the agentic loop can stall the fast execution, while a VLA that calls the agent on errors tends to re-trigger fallbacks repeatedly or completely miss silent failures. To address this, we propose REA (Routed Embodied Agent), a unified runtime where a deliberative System 2, an embodied router System 1 and reactive System 0 policies cooperate under a single golden rule - no running policy waits on the brain. Instead of querying the brain at each failure, REA employs a route memory that stores each policy's measured success ahead of time, a boundary-triggered judge gate that consults the brain on policy execution, and a shared-budget escalation ladder for bounded recovery. Evaluated on 3,000 test episodes across 60 LIBERO-Pro tasks, REA reaches 95.9% success and beats the best single-agent baseline in its own policy pool (94.7%) while reducing control steps by 25% and control time by 17%. In a separate 100-episode RoboTwin 2.0 study, REA achieves 84% success with VLA policies and 86% with VLA+code, compared with 80% for the strongest single VLA baseline MEDex. These results demonstrate learned and programmatic policies operating within the same runtime. REA also transfers to BEHAVIOR-1K and a real Unitree G1 humanoid, achieving the highest observed success among the compared methods.
[02.11.2026 09:37] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен REA (Routed Embodied Agent) — единая среда выполнения, где медленная LLM-модель System 2 отвечает за высокоуровневое рассуждение, router System 1 выбирает подходящую политику, а быстрые reactive-политики System 0 (VLA и контроллеры всего тела) выполняют действия. Ключевое правило: ни одна работающая политика не ждёт ответа «мозга», а координация выполняется асинхронно через route memory и judge gate с общим бюджетом эскалации. Метод протестирован на LIBERO-Pro, RoboTwin 2.0, BEHAVIOR-1K и humanoid-роботе Unitree G1, где он повышает успешность и сокращает число шагов управления на 25%. In a separate 100-episode RoboTwin 2.0 study, REA achieves 84% success with VLA policies and 86% with VLA+code, compared with 80% for the strongest single VLA baseline MEDex. These results demonstrate learned and programmatic policies operating within the same runtime. REA also transfers to BEHAVIOR-1K and a real Unitree G1 humanoid, achieving the highest observed success among the compared methods."

Abstract ReLift improves dense 3D tracking by repairing and completing lifted trajectories using geometric trajectory refinement without requiring image-based training.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Dense 3D tracking from monocular video requires establishing point correspondences over time, estimating their depth, and inferring their trajectories during occlusion. Existing methods typically learn these tasks jointly, a demanding objective given the scarcity of 4D supervision. We show that strong pretrained 2D trackers and 3D geometry models provide an effective foundation: lifted trajectories, with occluded segments simply interpolated, already rival dedicated 3D trackers using the same geometry. However, lifted trajectories inherit correspondence and depth errors, and under occlusion, their depth comes from the occluder rather than the tracked point. We introduce ReLift, an efficient feed-forward framework that recasts dense 3D tracking as geometric trajectory repair and completion. At its core, a trajectory corrector alternates attention along and across trajectories to preserve reliable observations, repair corrupted positions, and complete missing motion. Using only geometric inputs allows us to train directly on 3D trajectories with simulated lifting errors, decoupling training from images and from any specific tracker or geometry model. Concentrating 4D supervision on trajectory refinement yields a reusable model that consistently improves lifted trajectories across trackers and geometry providers without retraining, so it benefits directly from future advances in either component. On WorldTrack, ReLift achieves state-of-the-art mean accuracy, improving on the strongest baselines by 2.1 APD on all points and 3.0 on dynamic points, with faster configurations remaining competitive with leading 3D trackers while running faster than 4RC. Visit our supplementry page for more examples and code.

Из 2D-трекеров и 3D geometry models берутся траектории, а окклюзии заполняются интерполяцией, однако ошибки соответствий и глубины остаются. Ядро метода — trajectory corrector, который чередует attention вдоль и поперёк траекторий, исправляя искажённые позиции и восстанавливая пропущенное движение. Модель обучается только на геометрических данных с симулированными ошибками лифтинга, без изображений и привязки к конкретному трекеру, и на WorldTrack достигает state-of-the-art точности, опережая сильнейшие baselines.", "emoji": "🎯", "title": "Чинить и достраивать траектории вместо того, чтобы учить всё заново"}

Abstract Visual foundation models vary in supporting persistent spatial states across structure, persistence, prediction, and action, with readout accuracy not guaranteeing history-based updates or reliable goal-directed progress.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small An observer sees only part of the surrounding world, yet must keep track of what lies beyond the current view. Spatial perception therefore involves more than recognizing individual images: it calls for a persistent spatial state that is updated under self-motion and used to guide action. Do visual foundation models provide representations that support such a state? We study this question through four levels of spatial capability---spatial structure, object persistence, predictive spatial update, and actionability---using frozen-feature probes on ten representations under streaming egocentric observations of static indoor environments. The resulting capability profiles vary across tasks, network layers, and prefix lengths: strong readout of the visible scene does not ensure accurate object persistence or future-feature retrieval. Matched controls separate readout accuracy from reliance on visual history and motion. Native future prediction retains recoverable spatial structure, but a spatial reader trained on real features does not transfer to predicted features unchanged. Finally, goal-dependent candidate selection does not establish reliable continuous action utility: predicted actions respond to goal changes, yet supplying the correct goal does not reliably improve progress. Across the four levels, object persistence and actionability build on camera-pose readout, whereas feature prediction builds on visible-scene readout. Together, these findings motivate evaluating spatial representations not only by what can be decoded from them, but by whether that information is maintained beyond observation, updated predictively, and used for goal-directed action.

Abstract HALO introduces a human-annotated benchmark and dataset to evaluate proactive agents, revealing that over-proactivity is the dominant failure mode.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Proactive agents must decide whether, when, and how far to assist without an explicit request. Existing evaluations often rely on automated scores or binary verdicts, offering limited human diagnosis of evidence use and intervention quality. To address these gaps, we introduce HALO, Human-Grounded GrAnular EvaLuation of PrOactive Agents. HALO-Arena covers 4 proactive need types, Dependency, Blockage, Risk, and Opportunity, across 10 everyday domains and 50 personal environments. Scenarios require agents to retrieve evidence from structured databases or unstructured documents before judging whether assistance is warranted and choosing its scope. On HALO-Arena, we construct HALO-8K, a dataset of 8,000 responses from 10 models across 400 scenarios, annotated by 20 experts for action level, acceptability verdicts, and human diagnosis. For rejected responses, human diagnosis covers timing, intensity, and other issues. Recorded reasoning tokens and retrieval rounds measure the effort behind these responses. The best proprietary and open-source models achieve 72.88% and 56.63% acceptability, respectively. Our central finding is that over-proactivity is the dominant observed failure mode, with Premature timing or Excessive intensity accounting for 61.20% of rejected responses. Further analyses yield complementary findings on the balance between intervention and restraint, evidence acquisition, and the calibration of timing and intensity.

Abstract RoboArchitect automates robot harness design by iteratively revising control code around a frozen policy, substantially improving success on unseen scenes and tasks without model updates.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Recent work shows that a frozen robot policy performs far better inside an agent harness, where control code decides when to query the policy, consult vision tools, or command the arm directly. Yet today these harnesses are built by hand and redesigned for each new robot or benchmark. We show that harness design can be automated, offering a strong alternative to finetuning on a novel setup. In RoboArchitect, a coding model observes a robot's rollouts, diagnoses failures, and iteratively revises the harness around the frozen policy. The harness evolves on a set of episodes for its optimization and is evaluated on held-out scenes and tasks. Without updating any model weights, RoboArchitect raises average success from 9.7% to 43.3% on unseen scenes (4.5x) and from 12.7% to 40.2% on unseen tasks (3.2x), across multiple benchmarks and policies. The gains are largest where the benchmark is far from the policy's training data: wrapping a general-purpose policy, the evolved harness yields a specialist that often outperforms even the finetuned version. Intuitive control strategies are automatically designed, such as lifting a jammed arm clear or reaching in horizontally when an obstacle blocks the way from above. The harness also evolves its own persistent memory, carrying learned experience, such as effective grasp depths, into later decisions. These results suggest that substantial headroom in robotic manipulation lies in the agent harness, and its design can be effectively automated and optimized without weight updates.

Abstract WeFair improves personalized query rewriting via fidelity-aware human feedback stages—demonstration, preference, and verification—to reduce intent drift and boost search effectiveness in short-video platforms.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Short-video search needs compact query rewriters that recover users' personalized intent from ambiguous queries under strict latency constraints. Existing LLM-based methods have improved rewriting by using synthetic reasoning and subsequent consumption-behavioral feedback. However, overemphasizing subsequent consumption can favor engaging content over the user's immediate need, causing intent drift even when rewritten queries are grounded in behavioral history. We propose WeFair, Fidelity-Aware Alignment from Human Feedback for Personalized Query Rewriting in WeChat short-video search. WeFair uses human feedback according to how directly it reflects personalized intent, assigning feedback fidelity to a progression from demonstration to preference to verification. We first mine actual queries and user reformulations from search sessions, and then screen them for contextual support. In the demonstration stage, a supervised fine-tuning curriculum progresses from large-scale context-to-query learning to reasoning and direct prediction on human reformulation and preservation. In the preference stage, direct preference optimization learns relative preferences from subsequent consumption feedback. In the verification stage, group relative policy optimization rewards exact agreement with reformulation or preservation targets to constrain intent drift. Further analysis shows that broad supervision benefits the full curriculum supervised training, while strict verification achieves better performance than auxiliary rewards. In offline experiments, WeFair-2B outperforms strong rewriting baselines in overall accuracy and intent preservation. An online A/B test shows relative increases of 2.593% in effective video-view rate and a 2.350% relative reduction in query reformulation rate. WeFair-2B has been deployed in the WeChat short-video search system, which serves over 100 million queries per day.
[02.11.2026 09:37] Response: {"desc": "В статье предлагается WeFair, метод персонализированного переписывания поисковых запросов для коротких видео в WeChat, который использует human feedback с учётом степени точности его отражения намерения пользователя. Обучение проходит в три этапа: supervised fine-tuning на демонстрациях человеческих переформулировок, direct preference optimization по предпочтениям на основе последующего потребления контента и group relative policy optimization с наградой за точное совпадение с целевой переформулировкой. Такой подход снижает intent drift, когда модель выбирает вовлекающий контент вместо реальной потребности пользователя. "Abstract WeFair improves personalized query rewriting via fidelity-aware human feedback stages—demonstration, preference, and verification—to reduce intent drift and boost search effectiveness in short-video platforms.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Short-video search needs compact query rewriters that recover users' personalized intent from ambiguous queries under strict latency constraints. However, overemphasizing subsequent consumption can favor engaging content over the user's immediate need, causing intent drift even when rewritten queries are grounded in behavioral history. We propose WeFair, Fidelity-Aware Alignment from Human Feedback for Personalized Query Rewriting in WeChat short-video search. WeFair uses human feedback according to how directly it reflects personalized intent, assigning feedback fidelity to a progression from demonstration to preference to verification. In the verification stage, group relative policy optimization rewards exact agreement with reformulation or preservation targets to constrain intent drift. In offline experiments, WeFair-2B outperforms strong rewriting baselines in overall accuracy and intent preservation. An online A/B test shows relative increases of 2.593% in effective video-view rate and a 2.350% relative reduction in query reformulation rate. WeFair-2B has been deployed in the WeChat short-video search system, which serves over 100 million queries per day."

Abstract Venus-DefakerOne2 is a unified anti-forgery foundation model that extends detection to diverse forgery forms and media types through alternating LoRA and full-parameter tuning and entropy-based test adaptation.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Real-world forgery is inherently heterogeneous: it manifests not only as digital manipulation but also as physical spoofing, and is carried not only by images but also by videos. Despite substantial progress, a unified anti-forgery foundation model covering such a broad threat spectrum remains absent. In this paper, we present Venus-DefakerOne2, which substantially extends the forensic capability boundary along both the forgery-form and media-type dimensions. Trained on tens of millions of open-source and in-house samples, it incorporates two key designs. Tide-Style-Tuning alternates LoRA and full-parameter fine-tuning to balance computational efficiency, generalization, and hard-sample fitting while mitigating catastrophic forgetting. Entropy-based Test Adaptation determines the optimal prediction modality by analyzing prediction entropy. Venus-DefakerOne2 achieves new state-of-the-art performance across a comprehensive suite of benchmarks, including DeepFake faces (96.7%), AI-generated images (93.3%), tampered documents (87.6%), manipulated natural images (89.6%), physical spoofs (93.1%), and AI-generated videos (96.0%). It significantly outperforms both specialized expert models and its predecessor, DefakerOne, establishing itself as a powerful and versatile foundation model for binary-decision forensic tasks.

Abstract On-policy reinforcement learning for large language models suffers from training-inference mismatch due to numerical discrepancies and policy lag, and a derived discrepancy-lag correction stabilizes training by centering errors, clipping importance ratios, and resynchronizing sampling.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small On-policy reinforcement learning for large language models assumes that training responses are sampled from the current policy. In practice, an inference engine samples the responses and a separate trainer computes the gradients, and the training-inference mismatch between the two makes the training data off-policy. The mismatch has two sources, the numerics of the engine and the stale weights of asynchronous sampling, and a correction that stabilizes training under one source can fail under the other. We ask which property of a mismatch source decides the correction it needs. Evaluating the trainer at the sampler's weights splits each sampled-token error exactly into a numerical discrepancy of the engine and a policy lag of the trainer, and we follow both through training. The numerical discrepancy stays approximately constant, so it biases the updates by a nearly fixed amount, and centering the score at each prefix removes its leading bias. The policy lag is produced by the trainer's own updates: the bias it leaves enters the next update, which enlarges the next lag and closes a feedback loop. Importance weighting cuts this loop only while the lag ratio stays concentrated, and on Qwen3-1.7B it fails at the step where the effective sample size of the ratio falls below one half. From this account we derive the discrepancy-lag correction (DLC), which centers the numerical discrepancy, clips the lag ratio against the sampler's weights and resynchronizes the sampler when this effective sample size falls below one half. Under a stale sampler, DLC keeps the KL divergence from on-policy training within twice the KL between two independent on-policy trainings on Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3-1.7B, and its resynchronization prevents collapses that the per-token corrections leave and that a KL measured on the sampler's prefixes does not detect.
[02.11.2026 09:38] Response: {"desc": "Статья рассматривает проблему рассогласования между сэмплером (inference engine) и тренером в on-policy обучении с подкреплением для LLM: численные расхождения движка и устаревание весов при асинхронной генерации (policy lag) делают данные off-policy. Авторы показывают, что численная ошибка почти постоянна и смещает обновления, и её можно убрать центрированием score на каждом префиксе, тогда как policy lag образует обратную связь, которую importance weighting сдерживает лишь пока отношение весов остаётся сконцентрированным. На основе этого предложен метод DLC (discrepancy-lag correction): центрирование численной ошибки, клиппинг отношения importance weights и ресинхронизация сэмплера, когда effective sample size падает ниже одной второй. Эксперименты на Qwen3-0.6B и Qwen3-1.7B показывают, что DLC удерживает KL-дивергенцию в пределах удвоенного KL между двумя независимыми on-policy запусками и предотвращает коллапс обучения.", "emoji": "🔄", "title": "Синхронизируй сэмплер — и обучение LLM снова станет стабильным"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract On-policy reinforcement learning for large language models suffers from training-inference mismatch due to numerical discrepancies and policy lag, and a derived discrepancy-lag correction stabilizes training by centering errors, clipping importance ratios, and resynchronizing sampling.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small On-policy reinforcement learning for large language models assumes that training responses are sampled from the current policy. In practice, an inference engine samples the responses and a separate trainer computes the gradients, and the training-inference mismatch between the two makes the training data off-policy. The mismatch has two sources, the numerics of the engine and the stale weights of asynchronous sampling, and a correction that stabilizes training under one source can fail under the other. We ask which property of a mismatch source decides the correction it needs. Evaluating the trainer at the sampler's weights splits each sampled-token error exactly into a numerical discrepancy of the engine and a policy lag of the trainer, and we follow both through training. The numerical discrepancy stays approximately constant, so it biases the updates by a nearly fixed amount, and centering the score at each prefix removes its leading bias. The policy lag is produced by the trainer's own updates: the bias it leaves enters the next update, which enlarges the next lag and closes a feedback loop. Importance weighting cuts this loop only while the lag ratio stays concentrated, and on Qwen3-1.7B it fails at the step where the effective sample size of the ratio falls below one half. From this account we derive the discrepancy-lag correction (DLC), which centers the numerical discrepancy, clips the lag ratio against the sampler's weights and resynchronizes the sampler when this effective sample size falls below one half. Under a stale sampler, DLC keeps the KL divergence from on-policy training within twice the KL between two independent on-policy trainings on Qwen3-0.6B and Qwen3-1.7B, and its resynchronization prevents collapses that the per-token corrections leave and that a KL measured on the sampler's prefixes does not detect."

Abstract MemSteer generates evidence-grounded QA supervision from unlabeled user histories to optimize agent memory design without model updates, improving benchmark scores significantly.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small An effective agent memory system should adapt to a user's history and task requirements. Human-annotated supervision can guide this adaptation by providing feedback for evaluating and improving memory designs. Yet suitable labels are rarely available for new user histories, and manually constructing QA pairs and supporting evidence is costly. We introduce MemSteer, which turns unlabeled user histories into question-answer (QA) supervision with supporting evidence for memory-design optimization. MemSteer constructs an evidence-grounded answer plan that specifies how facts across related records jointly support a reference answer. The plan guides question generation, while source-grounded auditing and blind answer recovery check the resulting annotations. To study the utility of this supervision, we develop MemSteer-Opt, which combines QA-based design evaluation with evidence-guided failure diagnosis to revise memory store, retrieval, and context construction without updating model parameters. Experiments on ATM-Bench and RHELM show that this supervision is competitive with official-QA-guided optimization. Relative to Base-BM25, MemSteer-Opt improves ATM-Bench Question Score by 13.4 points and RHELM accuracy by 4.1 and 6.6 points without and with external data, respectively. The supervision also benefits two existing optimizers on ATM-Bench, while new-user experiments demonstrate continued design optimization using supervision generated from the target histories.

Система строит план ответа, связывающий факты из разных записей, а затем проверяет аннотации через аудит по источникам и слепое восстановление ответа. Abstract Causal World Action Models enforce sparse routing to isolate action-relevant latent variables, ensuring identifiable causal structure without requiring temporal independence assumptions.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small World action models (WAMs) learn latent variables from observations and use these to both predict the evolution of the scene and to generate actions. The prediction has to be of the whole scene, whereas only a portion of the scene causes actions. WAMs fitted to demonstrations do not, in general, guarantee that they will generate actions solely from the latent causes of the demonstrated actions (the action-relevant latent variables), as the model could just as well learn to imitate in this way whilst entangling the action-relevant latent variables with the nuisance variables. We therefore separate out what causes action from what prediction depends on. We prove that under a sparsity constraint the latent variables used to generate actions are an invertible function of the action-relevant latent variables alone and the true structure is identified up to a permutation of the latent variables. This requires neither sufficient temporal dependence nor any assumptions on how the unobserved regimes evolve and holds even when the action-relevant latent variables are statistically dependent on the rest. We therefore propose CWAM (Causal WAM), which incorporates the constraint into a pretrained WAM by adding a sparse routing path to its action head, with the world modeling objective remaining unchanged. Experiments on both synthetic data and real-world robots validate the theory as well as showcase the improved efficiency in closed-loop success.
[02.11.2026 09:38] Response: {"desc": "В статье предлагается Causal WAM (CWAM) — модель мира для действий, которая разделяет латентные переменные, влияющие на действия, и те, от которых зависит предсказание сцены. Авторы вводят ограничение разреженности (sparsity), которое направляет генерацию действий только через action-relevant латентные переменные. Доказано, что при таком ограничении истинная causal структура идентифицируема с точностью до перестановки переменных, без требований о временной зависимости. Эксперименты на синтетических данных и реальных роботах подтверждают теорию и показывают повышение эффективности closed-loop успешности.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Действия только из истинных причин: causal-структура для роботов"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Causal World Action Models enforce sparse routing to isolate action-relevant latent variables, ensuring identifiable causal structure without requiring temporal independence assumptions.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small World action models (WAMs) learn latent variables from observations and use these to both predict the evolution of the scene and to generate actions. The prediction has to be of the whole scene, whereas only a portion of the scene causes actions. WAMs fitted to demonstrations do not, in general, guarantee that they will generate actions solely from the latent causes of the demonstrated actions (the action-relevant latent variables), as the model could just as well learn to imitate in this way whilst entangling the action-relevant latent variables with the nuisance variables. We therefore separate out what causes action from what prediction depends on. We prove that under a sparsity constraint the latent variables used to generate actions are an invertible function of the action-relevant latent variables alone and the true structure is identified up to a permutation of the latent variables. This requires neither sufficient temporal dependence nor any assumptions on how the unobserved regimes evolve and holds even when the action-relevant latent variables are statistically dependent on the rest. We therefore propose CWAM (Causal WAM), which incorporates the constraint into a pretrained WAM by adding a sparse routing path to its action head, with the world modeling objective remaining unchanged. Experiments on both synthetic data and real-world robots validate the theory as well as showcase the improved efficiency in closed-loop success."

Abstract SciGame evaluates scientific discovery agents using structured cards with counterfactual environments to separate prior knowledge from empirical evidence, revealing that exact symbolic law recovery remains a key challenge.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Large language model agents are increasingly used for scientific discovery. However, existing benchmarks mix law inference with programming, score data fitting instead of prediction on unseen inputs, and do not separate empirical evidence from pretrained priors. We introduce SciGame, a controlled benchmark that converts scientific literature into structured scientific cards. Each card yields a pair of Original and dimensionally consistent Counterfactual environments with the same task interface. SciGame contains a Historical collection of canonical laws and a live Temporal collection curated from papers published after model knowledge cutoffs. It evaluates agents in candidate-guided Identification and open-ended Discovery settings. Each setting runs under Prior-only, Fixed, and Adaptive protocols. Experiments on five frontier models yield three findings. First, familiar priors hurt performance once the governing relation is altered. Second, adaptive experimentation helps the strongest models but harms weaker ones under limited budgets. Third, good explanations can hide quantitative failures. Recovering the exact symbolic form remains the main bottleneck, even when agents interpret evidence correctly. Current agents can investigate systems actively, but inducing exact laws in unfamiliar settings remains an open challenge.

Abstract GLOW-FM is a one-step latent flow-matching framework that enables efficient generative low-light image enhancement via single-function evaluation in a compact latent space.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Low-light image enhancement (LLIE) aims to recover well-exposed and perceptually faithful images from severely degraded observations. Recent diffusion-based generative methods achieve strong perceptual quality by leveraging powerful image priors, but their iterative sampling incurs substantial computational cost, limiting practical deployment. Despite recent efficiency gains in generative LLIE, high-quality enhancement with a single function evaluation and low overall computation remains challenging, particularly under diverse real-world low-light conditions. We introduce GLOW-FM, a one-step latent flow-matching framework for efficient generative LLIE. GLOW-FM performs conditional transport in the latent space of a frozen Tiny-VAE and leverages Terminal Velocity Matching (TVM) to enable enhancement with a single function evaluation. Matched comparisons with alternative one-step objectives support TVM as an effective design choice, and, to the best of our knowledge, this is its first adaptation to image restoration and specifically to LLIE. Extensive experiments on challenging real-world benchmarks demonstrate that GLOW-FM achieves competitive image quality and state-of-the-art cross-dataset generalization while requiring only a single function evaluation and 34 GFLOPs for the small variant. These findings highlight the potential of GLOW-FM to bring generative LLIE closer to practical deployment through single-step inference and compact latent modeling. Code will be made publicly available upon publication.
[02.11.2026 09:38] Response: {"desc": "GLOW-FM — это фреймворк на основе latent flow matching, который улучшает изображения при слабом освещении за один шаг. Модель выполняет условный перенос (conditional transport) в латентном пространстве замороженного Tiny-VAE, что делает обработку компактной. Для обучения используется Terminal Velocity Matching (TVM), который позволяет получать результат за одну function evaluation; это первая адаптация TVM для задач image restoration и LLIE. Abstract TernScope co-designs a compact ternary-weight segmentation network with simplified hardware to achieve high accuracy and fast inference for edge medical imaging.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Edge medical segmentation must reconcile small model size, high accuracy, and fast inference. TernScope addresses this tradeoff through model--hardware co-design, learning a compact segmentation network whose low-bit operators also simplify hardware execution. Split-channel large-kernel blocks preserve spatial context with limited computation, while branch fusion removes redundant inference paths. Ternary Progressive Quantization-aware Training (TPQ) then adapts the fused network from deep to shallow layers, through the decoder, and finally to the input and prediction layers. All 25 convolutions use ternary weights and 8-bit activations, enabling packed storage and multiplier-free computation. The resulting model uses 0.2483 M parameters and 0.0949 GMACs while retaining competitive segmentation accuracy on in-domain and external datasets. Under the same 45 nm technology, TernScope reduces compute-array area by 88.46% compared with the original Gemmini, a systolic-array accelerator with 8-bit times 8-bit multipliers. These results demonstrate a path from accurate lightweight medical segmentation to compact low-bit hardware.

Сеть использует тернарные веса (ternary weights) и 8-битные активации, что позволяет хранить модель в упакованном виде и вычислять без умножителей. Abstract SketchDirector is an agentic framework that decomposes text prompts into draft vector assets, arranges them via iterative reasoning with self-evolving memory, and refines details using diffusion priors for complex scene sketching.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Existing sketch generation methods often rely on image guidance and are largely limited to individual objects or simplified scenes, leaving text-guided complex scene sketching underexplored. We present SketchDirector, an agentic framework that mimics the human sketching workflow for automatic multi-concept scene sketching. Given a scene-level text prompt, SketchDirector first employs a large language model~(LLM) to decompose the scene into multiple draft assets, where each is represented by a set of Bézier curves. Then, an agentic reasoning loop iteratively plans and executes their spatial arrangement, guided by self-evolving memory. Finally, a detail refinement module leverages diffusion priors to enhance sketch quality while preserving geometric structure. To support future research and applications in multi-concept scene sketch generation, we further introduce the MCSketch8K dataset, comprising editable vector assets, compositional layouts, and object-level manipulation annotations. Extensive experiments show that SketchDirector outperforms state-of-the-art baselines in semantic and compositional quality while effectively preserving overall contour organization during refinement. The project page is: https://yiki77.github.io/SketchDirector/{https://yiki77.github.io/SketchDirector/}.

Большая языковая модель (LLM) планирует их пространственное расположение в итеративном цикле рассуждений с самообучающейся памятью (self-evolving memory). Затем модуль уточнения деталей использует диффузионные априорные знания (diffusion priors), чтобы улучшить качество эскиза и сохранить геометрическую структуру. Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small A voice agent's speech recognizer transcribes everyone near the microphone, including people other than the caller. I fine-tuned a public causal target-speaker extraction model (REAL-TSE, 27M parameters) on one GPU to work from a voice sample cut from the call itself, and released it as VoxLock. I score it by what a recognizer writes on real recordings. On Krisp's 265-recording benchmark it reaches 4.95% WER against 23.21% unprocessed and lets 9 background words into the transcript over 85 minutes of background-only speech. Its WER is not significantly different from Krisp's best filters and is significantly lower than the other commercial and open systems I tested. On 400 AMI meeting windows built and frozen before scoring, it cuts WER from 42.95% to 10.63%. The 8-bit model runs a live call on 77% of one CPU thread with 24 ms of algorithmic latency. Model, code and per-file scores are open.

Модель, названная VoxLock, подавляет речь посторонних людей, которую распознаватель иначе транскрибирует. На бенчмарке Krisp WER снижается с 23.21% до 4.95%, что сопоставимо с лучшими коммерческими фильтрами. Abstract Training acceleration in diffusion transformers is driven by reorganization of AdaLN-Zero residual gates, and a simple radial objective promoting broader gate participation improves generation quality without auxiliary teachers.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Training acceleration methods for diffusion transformers, such as REPA and dispersive regularization, are commonly explained by additional supervision, spatial-structure priors, or improved feature geometry. We revisit this view and uncover a striking internal change: the AdaLN-Zero residual gate α, which directly controls how conditioned updates enter the residual stream, is substantially reorganized by these methods. This observation motivates our hypothesis that acceleration arises, at least in part, from reshaping the residual gating pathway rather than solely from the feature structures explicitly imposed by auxiliary objectives. Supporting this view, accelerated models are markedly more sensitive to perturbations of α, and their gate geometry reflects semantic relationships among classes. We further introduce the normalized participation ratio (nPR) to characterize the channel-wise organization of the gates and find that, across REPA teachers, projector architectures, and radial-objective targets, broader gate participation accompanies better generation quality. Guided by this insight, we propose a simple teacher-free radial objective that promotes broader gate participation through feature-norm regularization, without pairwise feature comparisons. On ImageNet 256times256 with SiT-B/2, our method improves FID over dispersive loss while increasing mean gate participation over timesteps and classes, further supporting the link between gate organization and training acceleration. Our results identify residual-gate reorganization as a measurable axis of diffusion training acceleration and suggest a simple direction for further acceleration.
[02.11.2026 09:39] Response: {"desc": "Авторы показывают, что методы ускорения обучения диффузионных трансформеров (REPA, dispersive loss) заметно перестраивают AdaLN-Zero residual gate α, который управляет тем, как условные обновления попадают в residual stream. Для описания организации каналов гейтов вводится нормализованный participation ratio (nPR), и оказывается, что более широкое участие гейтов связано с лучшим качеством генерации. На основе этого предлагается простой teacher-free радиальный регуляризатор, который через норму признаков расширяет участие гейтов без попарных сравнений признаков. На ImageNet 256×256 с SiT-B/2 метод улучшает FID по сравнению с dispersive loss.", "emoji": "⚙️", "title": "Ускорение диффузии начинается с перестройки residual gates"}

"Abstract Training acceleration in diffusion transformers is driven by reorganization of AdaLN-Zero residual gates, and a simple radial objective promoting broader gate participation improves generation quality without auxiliary teachers.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Training acceleration methods for diffusion transformers, such as REPA and dispersive regularization, are commonly explained by additional supervision, spatial-structure priors, or improved feature geometry. We revisit this view and uncover a striking internal change: the AdaLN-Zero residual gate α, which directly controls how conditioned updates enter the residual stream, is substantially reorganized by these methods. This observation motivates our hypothesis that acceleration arises, at least in part, from reshaping the residual gating pathway rather than solely from the feature structures explicitly imposed by auxiliary objectives. Supporting this view, accelerated models are markedly more sensitive to perturbations of α, and their gate geometry reflects semantic relationships among classes. We further introduce the normalized participation ratio (nPR) to characterize the channel-wise organization of the gates and find that, across REPA teachers, projector architectures, and radial-objective targets, broader gate participation accompanies better generation quality. Guided by this insight, we propose a simple teacher-free radial objective that promotes broader gate participation through feature-norm regularization, without pairwise feature comparisons. On ImageNet 256times256 with SiT-B/2, our method improves FID over dispersive loss while increasing mean gate participation over timesteps and classes, further supporting the link between gate organization and training acceleration. Our results identify residual-gate reorganization as a measurable axis of diffusion training acceleration and suggest a simple direction for further acceleration."
